=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        self.r = len(pizza)
        self.c = len(pizza[0])
        
        # Step 1, pre-process the apple array and get the prefix sum
        tot_apples = 0
        self.pfsum_row = []
        self.pfsum_col = []
        
        for i in range(self.r):
            pfr = 0
            pfs_r = [0] * self.c
            pfs_c = [0] * self.c
            for j in range(self.c):
                if i > 0:
                    pfs_c[j] += self.pfsum_col[i - 1][j]
                if pizza[i][j] == 'A':
                    pfr += 1
                    pfs_c[j] += 1
                    tot_apples += 1
                pfs_r[j] = pfr
            self.pfsum_row.append(pfs_r)
            self.pfsum_col.append(pfs_c)
        
        if tot_apples < k:
            return 0
        
        if k = 1:
            return 1
        
        return self.getWays(0, 0, k) % (1000000007)
    
    
    @cache
    def getWays(self, i, j, k):
        if k == 1:
            # if only left one piece for cutting, we just need to check if there is any apple in the region
            found = False
            for c in range(j, self.c):
                apple_in_region = self.pfsum_col[self.r - 1][c]
                if i > 0:
                     apple_in_region -= self.pfsum_col[i - 1][c]
                if apple_in_region:
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                return 1
            return 0
        else:
            # horizontally cut
            cannot_cut = True
            nr = i
            t_cnt = 0
            while nr < self.r - 1:
                # find the first row that we can start cutting
                while nr < self.r - 1 and cannot_cut:
                    apple_in_region = self.pfsum_row[nr][self.c - 1]
                    if j > 0:
                        apple_in_region -= self.pfsum_row[nr][j - 1]
                    if apple_in_region:
                        cannot_cut = False
                    else:
                        nr += 1
                        
                if nr < self.r - 1:
                    t_cnt += self.getWays(nr + 1, j, k - 1)
                nr += 1
                
            # vertically cut
            cannot_cut = True
            nc = j
            while nc < self.c - 1:
                # find the first col that we can start cutting
                while nc < self. c - 1 and cannot_cut:
                    apple_in_region = self.pfsum_col[self.r - 1][nc]
                    if i > 0:
                        apple_in_region -= self.pfsum_col[i - 1][nc]
                    if apple_in_region:
                        cannot_cut = False
                    else:
                        nc += 1
                
                if nc < self.c - 1:
                    t_cnt += self.getWays(i, nc + 1, k - 1)
                nc += 1
            
            return t_cnt

    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        self.r = len(pizza)
        self.c = len(pizza[0])
        
        tot_apples = 0
        self.pfsum_row = []
        self.pfsum_col = []

            pfr = 0

```
@lru_cache(None)
def getWays(self, i, j, k):
    if k == 1:
        return 1 if self.has_apple(i, j, self.r-1, self.c-1) else 0
    ans = 0
    # horizontal cut: cut between row nr and nr+1, top piece rows i..nr
    for nr in range(i, self.r-1):
        if self.has_apple(i, j, nr, self.c-1):
            ans += self.getWays(nr+1, j, k-1)
    # vertical cut: cut between col nc and nc+1, left piece cols j..nc
    for nc in range(j, self.c-1):
        if self.has_apple(i, j, self.r-1, nc):
            ans += self.getWays(i, nc+1, k-1)
    return ans % MOD
```

Let's write fixed code:

```
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

MOD = 10**9 + 7

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        self.r = len(pizza)
        self.c = len(pizza[0])
        
        self.pref = [[0] * (self.c + 1) for _ in range(self.r + 1)]
        for i in range(self.r):
            for j in range(self.c):
                self.pref[i + 1][j + 1] = (
                    self.pref[i][j + 1]
                    + self.pref[i + 1][j]
                    - self.pref[i][j]
                    + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0)
                )
        
        if self.pref[self.r][self.c] < k:
            return 0
        
        return self.getWays(0, 0, k) % MOD

    def has_apple(self, top: int, left: int, bottom: int, right: int) -> bool:
        return (
            self.pref[bottom + 1][right + 1]
            - self.pref[top][right + 1]
            - self.pref[bottom + 1][left]
            + self.pref[top][left]
        ) > 0

    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def getWays(self, i: int, j: int, k: int) -> int:
        if k == 1:
            return 1 if self.has_apple(i, j, self.r - 1, self.c - 1) else 0
        
        ans = 0
        for row in range(i, self.r - 1):
            if self.has_apple(i, j, row, self.c - 1):
                ans += self.getWays(row + 1, j, k - 1)
        
        for col in range(j, self.c - 1):
            if self.has_apple(i, j, self.r - 1, col):
                ans += self.getWays(i, col + 1, k - 1)
        
        return ans % MOD
```

Need check if `getWays` with lru_cache and self.r/c attributes okay. If `ways` called multiple times on same Solution instance, self.pref changes, but lru_cache may have stale entries from previous call keyed by self. Since self is same object and attributes changed, cache could be wrong! This is important. If the same Solution instance is used for multiple test cases in LeetCode, `ways` may be called multiple times on the same instance? LeetCode creates a new Solution object for each test? Usually yes, but not guaranteed? In unit tests, they may instantiate once and call multiple times. If self.pref changes, cached getWays from previous call with same self would use old pref? Actually getWays uses self.pref at runtime, but cache key only self, i, j, k. If self.pref changed, cached result from previous call would be returned incorrectly. This is a subtle bug. Original code also had @cache on method and self.pref arrays built in ways; if same instance reused, stale cache. But LeetCode likely new instance. To be robust, avoid caching on self or clear cache at start of ways. We can define getWays as nested function inside ways with `@lru_cache`, capturing pref, r, c, MOD. That avoids self and stale cache across calls because new function each call. Better.

Let's consider using nested function. Code:

```
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

MOD = 10**9 + 7

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        r = len(pizza)
        c = len(pizza[0])
        pref = [[0]*(c+1) for _ in range(r+1)]
        ...
        def has_apple(top,left,bottom,right):
            return pref[bottom+1][right+1] - pref[top][right+1] - pref[bottom+1][left] + pref[top][left] > 0

        @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
        def dp(i,j,pieces):
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if has_apple(i,j,r-1,c-1) else 0
            ans = 0
            for row in range(i, r-1):
                if has_apple(i,j,row,c-1):
                    ans += dp(row+1,j,pieces-1)
            for col in range(j, c-1):
                if has_apple(i,j,r-1,col):
                    ans += dp(i,col+1,pieces-1)
            return ans % MOD

        if pref[r][c] < k:
            return 0
        return dp(0,0,k)
```

This is clean and avoids self. Need output only code. This is a bit more rewrite but within focused fix. The original method getWays removed. Is that okay? The prompt says fix code; can rewrite. Need ensure no bugs.

Need maybe decide final code style. Let's write:

```
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

MOD = 10**9 + 7

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        r = len(pizza)
        c = len(pizza[0])

        pref = [[0] * (c + 1) for _ in range(r + 1)]
        for i in range(r):
            for j in range(c):
                pref[i + 1][j + 1] = (
                    pref[i][j + 1] + pref[i + 1][j] - pref[i][j]
                    + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0)
                )

        def has_apple(top: int, left: int, bottom: int, right: int) -> bool:
            return (
                pref[bottom + 1][right + 1]
                - pref[top][right + 1]
                - pref[bottom + 1][left]
                + pref[top][left]
            ) > 0

        @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
        def dp(i: int, j: int, pieces: int) -> int:
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, c - 1) else 0

            ans = 0
            for row in range(i, r - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, row, c - 1):
                    ans += dp(row + 1, j, pieces - 1)

            for col in range(j, c - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, col):
                    ans += dp(i, col + 1, pieces - 1)

            return ans % MOD

        if pref[r][c] < k:
            return 0

        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
actually contains code? The final answer should be code. I'll output raw code. Ensure no extra prose.

Need maybe include imports. Final code:

```
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

MOD = 10**9 + 7

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        r = len(pizza)
        c = len(pizza[0])

        pref = [[0] * (c + 1) for _ in range(r + 1)]
        for i in range(r):
            for j in range(c):
                pref[i + 1][j + 1] = (
                    pref[i][j + 1] + pref[i + 1][j] - pref[i][j]
                    + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0)
                )

        def has_apple(top: int, left: int, bottom: int, right: int) -> bool:
            return (
                pref[bottom + 1][right + 1]
                - pref[top][right + 1]
                - pref[bottom + 1][left]
                + pref[top][left]
            ) > 0

        @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
        def dp(i: int, j: int, pieces: int) -> int:
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, c - 1) else 0

            ans = 0
            for row in range(i, r - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, row, c - 1):
                    ans += dp(row + 1, j, pieces - 1)

            for col in range(j, c - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, col):
                    ans += dp(i, col + 1, pieces - 1)

            return ans % MOD

        if pref[r][c] < k:
            return 0

        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
```

Need ensure no issue with `MOD` if judge doesn't allow global? It's okay. If they only want class, top-level imports/constants okay. If they don't want imports? no.

Let's think if `lru_cache` on nested dp with `has_apple` closure and `pref` list; no issue.

Potential issue: If `pref[r][c] < k`, return0. If `k` is 1 and no apples, return0. If `k` is 1 and apples, dp returns1. Good.

from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

MOD = 10**9 + 7

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        r = len(pizza)
        c = len(pizza[0])

        pref = [[0] * (c + 1) for _ in range(r + 1)]
        for i in range(r):
            for j in range(c):
                pref[i + 1][j + 1] = (
                    pref[i][j + 1] + pref[i + 1][j] - pref[i][j]
                    + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0)
                )

        def has_apple(top: int, left: int, bottom: int, right: int) -> bool:
            return (
                pref[bottom + 1][right + 1]
                - pref[top][right + 1]
                - pref[bottom + 1][left]
                + pref[top][left]
            ) > 0

        @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
        def dp(i: int, j: int, pieces: int) -> int:
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, c - 1) else 0

            ans = 0
            for row in range(i, r - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, row, c - 1):
                    ans += dp(row + 1, j, pieces - 1)

            for col in range(j, c - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, col):
                    ans += dp(i, col + 1, pieces - 1)

            return ans % MOD

        if pref[r][c] < k:
            return 0

        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
